In the 1980s, the hand was planted in a field of 50,000 acres, producing 350-400,000 tonnes of sugar beets, while 6,500 farmers dealt with sugar beets. In 2009, the quota was set for 200,000 tonnes, the promised 150,000 tonnes, the number of farmers 2,513, and the number of villages with sugar beets is 77; in 2010, there were 128,000 tonnes, the number of farmers promised 1,921, 68; in 2011, the quota was 210,000 tonnes, and the number of farmers was 1.753, 65 and 13,000 hectares, and the number of villages were added in 65 and 13,000 hectares. In recent years, the number of farmers dealing with sugar beets has significantly decreased. With the privatisation of the Elazig Candy Factory, the promised production for 2012 will not take place, and the economy of our province will be severely damaged. Sugar beets should not be thought to be used only in sugar production. The sugar beets are used as an important bait for the age and dry-faced animalism, and the reduction in sugar beets has a negative effect on animalism. Because of the quota, farmers pray that there will be no more production than they sow. Because when they produce more than the amount they're committed to, the commitment surplus will be bought for half the price for production, and it will be paid in six months. The increase in input prices, the lack of fuel and fertilizer support, because their water was cut due to energy debt, farmers failed to produce what they were committed to and were executed through factory lawyers. It's not enough that farmers can't produce because of the wrong practices, and they're struggling to make a living, but they're now engaged in execution. The sugar beets are also one of the basic raw materials that can be used in bioethanol production known as fuel for the future, while high levels of industrial input (power, medicine, mechanization etc.) It's a plant that requires use. Therefore, it is an important agricultural product that provides the most support for the development of the side sectors. The production of sugar beets in our province has an indirect effect on about 60,000 people. Farmers who can't produce sugar beets and can't make a living in their village migrate to the city centre and can't adapt to social life. It leads to deviant urbanization and increased unemployment. The sugar industry, which provides the most jobs from its production to its consumption, has not been supported enough in our country in recent years. The price of input used in production has increased several times over inflation, while sugar beets purchase prices have decreased year-on-year. In 2000, a farmer who produces 100 tonnes of sugar beets buys a tractor, while a farmer who produces 100 tonnes of sugar beets in 2011 is able to pay for its costs and labor. In our Elazig province, to restore sugar beets production, farmers must be given diesel, fertilizer, medicine and seed support, equipped with new technology, the sugar factory must be powered with sugar beets, and the quota must be removed. In order to investigate the problems of the sugar beets producers, which have contributed significantly to our country's economy, and the measures needed to improve the economic and prosperity of the people living in the region, we propose a parliamentary investigation of the Constitution under Articles 104 and 105. tbmm-2013-07-09sit02spe0183par0498	Thank you. First of all, I'm going to try to give you a brief answer to questions about the recruitment of contract personnel. Under Article 657 of Law 4/B, contractors in the local administrations, under contract health staff under Law 4924, are assigned to a contract staff based on the laws of the organization; this is the scope. Under the relevant legislation, there must be a condition for re-employment, a contracted study based on an employment or visa-winning position. On the other hand, such as CSIs, such as COSGEB, such as the Bank of Provinces, are not within the scope of all contracting employees. However, partial employees will not be part-time. I mean, that's basically the answer to the question of who's not who. Another question is: is there a question such as 3600 additional indicators for groups that work, such as lawyers, policemen, university secretary general? These personnel' additional indicator increases can only be addressed with general regulations for additional indicator regulations, and so far our experience has been: Last time, especially with the same amount of work or equal work that we've done under the rule of law, it's an important regulation in the public sector, but there are very different laws that are subject to about 3 million people working in the public. So it's not really easy to find a 100 percent ideal system in practice, but our efforts for this purpose have been so far, and when the right demands come, they will undoubtedly be evaluated by our relevant units. There were questions about the Travel Park. Both our governorship and all our relevant public units are acting within the principles of the Travel Park, or of different events in various parts of Turkey, in various cities. What are these principles? First of all, basic rights, fundamental freedoms, which are very, very clear in our laws, but on the other hand, as a legal state of law, as a required state of law, and at the same time, with the notification of these decisions and the writing of these decisions to the relevant units, the units are undoubtedly implementing these decisions, whatever the judicial decision is. But you know the verdicts, step by step, there's phase one, then there's the Supreme Courts. So in the end, whatever is necessary in a legal state, within the framework of the independence of the judiciary and within the framework of the division of powers, must be done and will be done in the following period. But on the other hand, there is no tolerance for violence. When violence is involved, when it comes to damage to our citizens, our security forces will surely fulfill their duties. There is no longer any subject to consider in this bag about the Law of Disruption, but there is no doubt that future problems with implementation can always be addressed, problems can always be reviewed, steps may be taken, but for today, any initiative of ours as a government will not be involved. Besides, I had some questions about the arrests and arrests, and I don't have any information about them right now, but from our concerned friends, especially our Home Secretary, the assurance of these information is at stake at any moment. There was another question about our Central Bank. You know, our Central Bank's main priority in the framework of operational independence is price stability, and our Central Bank maintains its policies on other priorities, provided we don't contradict its price stability goal, but financial stability is something that our Central Bank contributes and strives to strengthen. In terms of financial stability, the Central Bank is not our only institution, and other institutions do the necessary intervention when necessary. This has come up a few times before, and we've been asked in semesters. In particular, there has been significant activity in the markets of all developing countries since May 22nd. This is not just a special movement for Turkey, Brazil, India, Russia, there is movement in the markets of many developing countries that you can think of. In particular, the decisions made by the Central Bank of America, the Central Bank of Europe and the Central Bank of Japan and its decisions are important for many countries to review their own monetary policies and other economic policies. These developments will create new balances in many developing countries. The balances of these pre-May 22nd and after 22 May will surely be different, these balances will find itself in different market indicators, but we don't see a serious problem that will be concerned for us because Turkey's already prepared for this issue in time by all of our institutions. Thank you.
